## How to read this report

This report uses three different scales. Each answers a different question, and they can disagree on the same unit — that is expected, not a contradiction.

- **Confidence** (HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW, per unit below) — how sure the matched rule is about *this* unit's configuration. Answers: *is this specific mapping decision reliable?*
- **Feasibility** (SUPPORTED/CONDITIONAL/UNSUPPORTED/UNKNOWN, per unit below) — whether the source configuration can be represented on STACKIT at all, and how cleanly. Answers, together with Confidence: *is this mapping usable today?*
- **Target maturity** (GA/BETA/EXPERIMENTAL, shown only when not GA) — a STACKIT provider notice about the target Terraform resource itself. Answers: *will the STACKIT provider change this resource under me, independently of whether the mapping is right?*

This report states each decision, its reason and what you have to do. The working behind it — the semantic surface and property accounting of every unit, every critical-property entry, and the engine's own text for a decision that depends on relations it could not resolve — is in `engineering/analysis-details.md` next to this file; `assessment.json` has all of it in machine-readable form.

- **Production suitability: SUITABLE.** Suitable as a landing: 1.36. Review before use: none. Not suitable as a landing (ended or ending inside 30 days): 1.34, 1.33, 1.32. No date evidence, so not assessed: 1.35.
- **Upgrade action.** Upgrade before the expiry: none. Plan the upgrade (an end is announced): 1.36. Already expired, the provider's mandatory update applies: 1.34, 1.33, 1.32. No end published, nothing due that the sources show: none.
- **Migration timing risk** (that the end of the landing version falls inside the migration). HIGH: 1.34, 1.33, 1.32. MEDIUM: none. LOW: 1.36. UNKNOWN (an unpublished end is not safety): 1.35.
- **Day-2 responsibility.** When a version reaches its expiry the update is performed by the provider (STACKIT): "If the Kubernetes or OS version of a cluster has reached its expiration date, SKE starts a mandatory update to the highest available patch version of the current minor version, or to the highest patch version of the consecutive minor version that is not classified as preview version." and "mandatory version updates run, even if the auto update for the Kubernetes version is deactivated". Automatic updates otherwise "only update to higher patch levels" in the cluster's maintenance window. Notice: "As a SKE user you will get informed via release notes and via email in case you are operating a cluster with a deprecated Kubernetes version." Customer: "you can check the exact expiration dates for your current Kubernetes and node pool versions in the cluster.status.expiration field via the SKE API". The page does not assign any further duty (workload readiness for the update, testing) to the customer or to STACKIT: not stated.
- **1.36:** The expiry day of 1.36 was not published on 2026-10-08 and is on 2026-11-03; the page has no release column, so the release date is still the one of the release note.
- **1.35:** The pages read give no release date for 1.35 (no release-note entry), so the state is not known: a future expiry date does not say that a version is offered. The 1.34 deprecation entry names 1.35 as the upgrade target, and the SKE API catalogue that ships with this build lists 1.35.8 as supported; the draft of the Kubernetes version reads that catalogue for a row like this one, so a release date is not estimated here.
- **1.34:** Clusters still on 1.34 are upgraded to 1.35 by the mandatory update (release note of 2026-08-28); the day it runs inside the maintenance window is not stated, so no forced-update date is recorded.
- **1.33:** The expiry day differs between the lifecycle page (2026-06-10) and the release note (2026-07-08); the version is past its end of support under either.
- **1.32:** No release date for 1.32 appears in the pages read.

The 30-day window that makes a version EXPIRING is a StackToIT presentation parameter, not a provider fact.

Sources (read 2026-11-03): https://docs.stackit.cloud/products/runtime/kubernetes-engine/basics/operations/version-updates/; https://docs.stackit.cloud/products/runtime/kubernetes-engine/reference/lifecycle/; https://docs.stackit.cloud/products/runtime/kubernetes-engine/release-notes/.

### A data source, read when a plan runs — 5 unit(s)

**Remedy.** The deciding data source is read when a plan runs, so only a plan resolves it. Produce a plan in your environment (`tofu plan -out p && tofu show -json p > plan.json`): a plan would resolve this value, but StackToIT does not read plans.

**Source constraint.** 5 of these unit(s) have a count that rests on a call the source may not be valid for, so it is an interval, never an exact number (POSSIBLE_SOURCE_ERROR: `slice(…, 3)` fails in Terraform and OpenTofu when the list has fewer than 3 elements (the source proves none), so the count is not exact). A plan that succeeds settles it.

### A root variable that a variable file or the command line can override — 3 unit(s)

**Remedy.** Only the default of a root variable decides whether this exists on the defaults (a default of 0, false or an empty set switches it off) and how often, and a default is not a fact of the source: a variable file, `-var` or `TF_VAR_<name>` can change it, so it is never read as existing now. Give the variable its value — `--var-file FILE`, or a `terraform.tfvars` / `*.auto.tfvars` next to the source — and assess again; StackToIT then reads that value as the source's.

- **Critical semantics left to you to decide or accept (19):** service-connect-values, service-desired-count, service-discovery, service-execute-command, service-network-placement, service-public-ip-setting, service-task-definition, task-definition and 11 more (engineering/analysis-details.md) (**not automation-eligible**: no property the unit sets is read by linked-dependency:aws_dynamodb_table, service-discovery)
- **Property accounting:** 24 of 24 meaningful properties accounted
- **20 critical setting(s) left to you** — **not automation-eligible**:
  - network exposure (10; REVIEW 10): `network_configuration.assign_public_ip`, `network_configuration.security_groups`, `network_configuration.subnets`, `service_connect_configuration.enabled`, `service_connect_configuration.namespace` and 5 more — NOT CARRIED (module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_service.this): network_configuration — the tasks have their own network interface in the subnets and security groups of this block (awsvpc); pods use the cluster network and the security group rules are not translated into network policies — decide the network policy of the pods.
  - identity access (4; REVIEW 4): `enable_execute_command`, `task_definition`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_task_definition.this.execution_role_arn`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_task_definition.this.task_role_arn` — NOT CARRIED (module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_service.this): enable_execute_command = true, tags, wait_for_steady_state = true — a service setting with no counterpart in the draft manifests: Kubernetes has its own rollout (maxSurge, maxUnavailable), health, scheduling and exec behaviour, and tags are not labels here.
  - availability durability (1; REVIEW 1): `desired_count`
  - secret handling (1; REVIEW 1): `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_task_definition.this.container_definitions`
  - workload compatibility (4; REVIEW 4): `launch_type`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_task_definition.this.cpu`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_task_definition.this.memory`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_task_definition.this.requires_compatibilities` — NOT CARRIED (module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_service.this): launch_type = FARGATE — the ECS launch type and the Fargate platform version are AWS placement and runtime choices; the pods run on the SKE node pool (no per-task isolation boundary, no platform version, no Fargate sizing steps).
- **Why those are open, and what closes them:**
  - `service-discovery`: A check of the knowledge base (ecs service discovery) fired on this unit and leaves the setting to a person: the target does not settle it by itself (a finding of this unit describes it). The setting states how ECS services find each other: AWS Cloud Map registries and namespaces, or ECS Service Connect. Closes when: A person decides it as that finding asks, and the finding's closing evidence or decision is recorded. A person replaces the lookups with Kubernetes Services and cluster DNS on SKE.
  - `task-definition`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The ECS task definition: the containers a service runs, with their CPU and memory, environment, secrets and health checks. Closes when: A person translates the task definition into Kubernetes Deployments and Services on SKE (CPU and memory, environment, secrets and health checks become manifest fields); the rule names a Cloud Foundry push as the other way for a simple web app.
  - `service-task-definition`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The task definition the service runs (family:revision or ARN) becomes the Deployment's pod template; ecs_service.task_definition names it as a string, the definition itself is a separate resource a person translates. Closes when: A person confirms the value against the target, or the knowledge base gains a rule that decides this setting.
  - `service-desired-count`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. desired_count is the number of tasks ECS keeps running (ecs_service docs: 'Number of instances of the task definition to place and keep running'); on SKE it becomes Deployment replicas or a HorizontalPodAutoscaler floor. The draft writes it as the replicas of the Deployment in kubernetes/<service>/manifests.yaml when it is a literal number (a HorizontalPodAutoscaler owns the count where the service has a scaling target and policy the draft translates; a number that is not visible statically is a REQUIRED_ token) — a person confirms the number. Closes when: A person confirms the value against the target, or the knowledge base gains a rule that decides this setting.
  - `service-execute-command`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. ECS Exec opens a shell in a running task; kubectl exec (and who may use it through RBAC) is the counterpart and is not part of the draft. Closes when: A person confirms the value against the target, or the knowledge base gains a rule that decides this setting.
  - `service-network-placement`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The subnets and security groups the tasks' network interfaces use (awsvpc mode); on SKE pods take the cluster's network and NetworkPolicies decide reachability — a person re-expresses the security group rules. Closes when: A person confirms the value against the target, or the knowledge base gains a rule that decides this setting.
  - and 13 kinds of reason more (engineering/analysis-details.md)
- **Review:**
  - [ ] Translate task definitions (CPU/memory, env, secrets, health checks) into Kubernetes manifests.

#### `module.dependencies.module.dynamodb_carts.aws_dynamodb_table.this`

- **Source:** `aws_dynamodb_table` — .terraform/modules/dependencies.dynamodb_carts/main.tf:1
- **Module:** `module.dependencies.module.dynamodb_carts` (terraform-aws-modules/dynamodb-table/aws 3.3.0)
- **Candidate target:** STACKIT MongoDB Flex (alternative — data-model redesign; PostgreSQL Flex JSONB is the other option) (`stackit_mongodbflex_instance`)
- **Target maturity:** GA · lifecycle active · offered in eu01, eu02 (live catalogue snapshot)
- **Classification:** REDESIGN · **Confidence:** MEDIUM · rule `aws.dynamodb_table/dynamodb`
- **Feasibility:** CONDITIONAL — blocked by a feature the draft does not carry yet (semantic key-schema, semantic index-projection-and-name, semantic encryption-at-rest, semantic point-in-time-recovery and 4 more); a point the scan could not establish
- **Why:** There is no DynamoDB-compatible service on STACKIT. The access layer is rewritten against a document store (MongoDB Flex) or relational JSONB (PostgreSQL Flex); key design, capacity modes and conditional writes do not carry over. The draft is an alternative, not an equivalent: one MongoDB Flex instance for the estate's tables, each table a collection with its keys and secondary indexes named as MongoDB indexes.
- **Deployment prerequisites:** passed StackToIT's own draft checks; tofu validate: PASSED; NOT deployment-ready: 1 open decisions, 0 manual actions
  - decision `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-DATA-MOVEMENT/module.dependencies.module.dynamodb_carts.aws_dynamodb_table.this` (alias `convert:module.dependencies.module.dynamodb_carts.aws_dynamodb_table.this:data:DATA_MOVEMENT`): Choose how the data of module.dependencies.module.dynamodb_carts.aws_dynamodb_table.this moves from aws_dynamodb_table to STACKIT MongoDB Flex (stackit_mongodbflex_instance.dynamodb_tables): the method, the downtime it needs and the check that the copy is complete · StackToIT moves no data and writes no runbook for aws_dynamodb_table to STACKIT MongoDB Flex; the data-movement register lists module.dependencies.module.dynamodb_carts.aws_dynamodb_table.this with the mechanism UNKNOWN, and the source states neither the size of the data nor the downtime the business tolerates nor the consistency the application needs
- **INFO** (dynamodb-secondary-indexes): Secondary indexes become MongoDB indexes on the collection; projections and the eventual consistency of global secondary indexes do not carry over.
- **WARNING** (source-provider-unpinned): No lock file entry pins aws (>= 3.0, >= 3.29, >= 4.18, >= 4.30, >= 4.59, >= 5.64, >= 5.79, ~> 5.0): the version the plan would use is not proven, so the knowledge base (checked against 5.100.0) is not taken as proof of it (stated once for the whole estate, with the units it covers and the rest of the words)
- **Critical semantics left to you to decide or accept (6):** key-schema, index-projection-and-name, encryption-at-rest, point-in-time-recovery, ttl, tc-dynamodb-table-name
- **Open choice(s) the draft could not make:** `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-DATA-MOVEMENT/module.dependencies.module.dynamodb_carts.aws_dynamodb_table.this` (alias `convert:module.dependencies.module.dynamodb_carts.aws_dynamodb_table.this:data:DATA_MOVEMENT`) — the source does not prove what it means and the draft holds a required variable without a default; a person decides, and until then the unit is **not automation-eligible**.
- **Property accounting:** 13 of 13 meaningful properties accounted
- **11 critical setting(s) left to you** — **not automation-eligible**:
  - encryption (1; REVIEW 1): `server_side_encryption.enabled`
  - backup retention (3; REVIEW 3): `point_in_time_recovery.enabled`, `ttl.attribute_name`, `ttl.enabled`
  - workload compatibility (2; REVIEW 2): `global_secondary_index.name`, `name`
  - data semantics (5; REVIEW 5): `attribute.name`, `attribute.type`, `global_secondary_index.hash_key`, `global_secondary_index.projection_type`, `hash_key` — TABLE module.dependencies.module.dynamodb_carts.aws_dynamodb_table.this (.terraform/modules/dependencies.dynamodb_carts/main.tf:1) → collection "retail-store-ecs-carts": key id → _id (or a unique index on id); GSI idx_global_customerId {customerId: 1} → secondary index (strongly consistent in MongoDB; the projection is not needed); GSI projections and capacities → NOT CARRIED (a MongoDB index finds whole documents; capacity is flavor sizing); billing mode PAY_PER_REQUEST → NOT CARRIED (flavor sizing).
- **Why those are open, and what closes them:**
  - `key-schema`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The draft names the partition/sort key as the collection's unique index; the data model is rewritten by people. Closes when: A person designs the keys and indexes of the replacement collection (partition and sort key, attribute names and types, secondary index keys), because the draft names only the key as the collection's unique index.
  - `index-projection-and-name`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The projection of a secondary index (KEYS_ONLY, INCLUDE with its non_key_attributes, ALL) decides which attributes an index query can return, and the index name is what every Query / Scan on the index selects it by (API Query IndexName). STACKIT has no provider resource for an index (pinned provider), a person re-creates the indexes and the queries that name them in the chosen engine. Closes when: A person confirms the value against the target, or the knowledge base gains a rule that decides this setting.
  - `encryption-at-rest`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. It is not stated in stackit_mongodbflex_instance (no encryption attribute) whether STACKIT MongoDB Flex encrypts data at rest: review (the customer managed key setting reads the key). Closes when: A person confirms the value against the target, or the knowledge base gains a rule that decides this setting.
  - `point-in-time-recovery`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. DynamoDB restores a table to any point in time within its recovery period (1 to 35 days; 35 when the table states none). A recovery that is off, one day (24 hours) or not known stays left for review: the draft is an alternative, not an equivalent, and a person compares the recovery the application needs. Closes when: A person accepts the 30-hour window in writing, or designs protection of their own for the older hours (work the draft does not generate) and checks a restore against it.
  - `ttl`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. DynamoDB TTL reads epoch seconds; a MongoDB TTL index expires only BSON dates, so the attribute changes type. The time-to-live check still fires only for enabled = true; the case handler review covers the other states. Closes when: A person confirms the value against the target, or the knowledge base gains a rule that decides this setting.
  - `tc-dynamodb-table-name`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. aws_dynamodb_table.name: Held critical: the workload, its clients or its neighbours rely on it (an address, a name, a protocol or an execution contract). Closes when: A person confirms the name the replacement collection carries on the target against what refers to the source name, or accepts the new name.
- **Review:**
  - [ ] Rewrite the data access layer against the MongoDB API (queries, conditional writes, transactions).
  - [ ] Size the instance from the tables' data volume and request load; capacity units do not translate.
- **Evidence** (validated against STACKIT product catalogue 2026-09-23 (no managed key-value NoSQL with a DynamoDB-compatible API); terraform-provider-stackit@0.116.0 (stackit_mongodbflex_instance: flavor, storage, replicas, backup schedule, point-in-time window; no encryption key attribute); STACKIT catalogue eu01 2026-09-24; docs.stackit.cloud MongoDB Flex pages Backup, restore and clone and Architecture (both last updated May 22, 2026, read 2026-11-02: the point-in-time window is 30 hours and fixed); docs.aws.amazon.com DynamoDB pages Point-in-time recovery for DynamoDB and PointInTimeRecoverySpecification (read 2026-11-02: recovery period 1 to 35 days, restore to any point in time within it, 35 when none is stated); found compatible through 0.121.0 by the release check): https://docs.stackit.cloud/products/databases/mongodb-flex/ · https://docs.stackit.cloud/products/databases/mongodb-flex/how-tos/backup-restore-and-clone/ · https://docs.stackit.cloud/products/databases/mongodb-flex/basics/architecture/ · https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/PointInTimeRecovery_Howitworks.html · https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/APIReference/API_PointInTimeRecoverySpecification.html · https://registry.terraform.io/providers/stackitcloud/stackit/0.116.0/docs/resources/mongodbflex_instance

- **WARNING** (aurora-storage-encrypted-mariadb): Storage is encrypted at rest with an AWS-managed key. STACKIT MariaDB lists "data-at-rest encryption" as a feature, but a sandbox instance (MariaDB 10.11, 2026-09-24) had database-level encryption off (innodb_encrypt_tables, innodb_encrypt_log, encrypt_binlog, aria_encrypt_tables = OFF); whether the volumes beneath are encrypted cannot be observed from a client and is not stated: no STACKIT fact is documented, so it is not known and is not relied on.
- **WARNING** (aurora-mysql-mariadb-auth): The source engine is MySQL 8 and its accounts do not move. MySQL 8.0's default authentication plugin is caching_sha2_password (system variable default_authentication_plugin), MariaDB 10.11's default is mysql_native_password or unix_socket; users created with MySQL's SHA256 password algorithm cannot be used in MariaDB 10.11 (MDEV-9804), and a caching_sha2_password plugin exists in MariaDB Community Server only from 11.4.9, 11.8.4 and 12.1 and is not installed by default, while STACKIT offers MariaDB 10.11 (catalogue 2026-10-18). Create the accounts on the target and check that every client library authenticates with a plugin MariaDB 10.11 offers: a client setting that requires caching_sha2_password cannot connect.
- **WARNING** (aurora-mysql-mariadb-json): MariaDB stores JSON as text, not in a binary format as MySQL: its JSON data type is an alias for LONGTEXT, so the JSON columns of the source become text columns and JSON values are compared as strings, where MySQL compares them as JSON values. MariaDB 10.11 does not support MySQL's JSON operators -> and ->> (MDEV-13594): a query that uses them is rewritten with the JSON functions (JSON_EXTRACT, JSON_UNQUOTE); JSON_SEARCH given invalid data returns NULL and a warning where MySQL raises an error.
- **WARNING** (aurora-mysql-mariadb-collation): The defaults of the character set and the collation differ: MySQL 8.0 uses utf8mb4 and utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci, MariaDB 10.11 uses latin1 or utf8 and latin1_swedish_ci or utf8_general_ci, and MariaDB names utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci as the counterpart default only from 11.4. Not all character sets and collations exist in both products, and the pages read do not list utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci as a MariaDB 10.11 collation: check every collation the schema, the dump and the queries name with SHOW COLLATION on the target, and set the character set of every database explicitly.
- **WARNING** (aurora-mysql-mariadb-sql-mode): The default sql_mode differs: MySQL 8.0 sets ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY, STRICT_TRANS_TABLES, NO_ZERO_IN_DATE, NO_ZERO_DATE, ERROR_FOR_DIVISION_BY_ZERO and NO_ENGINE_SUBSTITUTION; MariaDB 10.11 sets STRICT_TRANS_TABLES, ERROR_FOR_DIVISION_BY_ZERO, NO_AUTO_CREATE_USER and NO_ENGINE_SUBSTITUTION. Without ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY the GROUP BY semantics the queries rely on are not enforced the way MySQL enforces them, and zero dates and zero parts in dates are no longer refused by default. A parameter group of the source may set sql_mode itself, and whether the STACKIT instance lets sql_mode be set is not established here: test the queries and the inserts against the target.
- **INFO** (aurora-mysql-mariadb-queries): Common table expressions (non-recursive and recursive) and window functions are supported by both products (window functions since MariaDB 10.2, per MariaDB's compatibility matrix), so no incompatibility is documented for them; the queries are tested anyway. Documented differences in queries: MariaDB 10.11 does not support lateral derived tables (MDEV-19078) or the MAX_EXECUTION_TIME optimizer hint, and the function VALUES() is called VALUE(); INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS has slightly different fields and the role views differ; the performance schema is off by default in MariaDB 10.11 and on in MySQL. Generated columns are compatible: tables created with MySQL 5.7 or later that hold generated columns import into MariaDB, and STORED is an alias of PERSISTENT.
- **WARNING** (source-provider-unpinned): No lock file entry pins aws (>= 3.0, >= 3.29, >= 4.18, >= 4.30, >= 4.59, >= 5.64, >= 5.79, ~> 5.0): the version the plan would use is not proven, so the knowledge base (checked against 5.100.0) is not taken as proof of it (stated once for the whole estate, with the units it covers and the rest of the words)

#### `module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default`

- **Source:** `aws_elasticache_replication_group` — .terraform/modules/dependencies.checkout_elasticache_redis/main.tf:115
- **Module:** `module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis` (cloudposse/elasticache-redis/aws 0.53.0)
- **Candidate target:** STACKIT Key Value Store (Valkey) (`stackit_valkey_instance`, `stackit_valkey_credential`)
- **Target maturity:** GA · lifecycle active · offered in eu01, eu02 (live catalogue snapshot)

- **Why:** Redis/Valkey caches move to STACKIT Key Value Store (Valkey, GA since August 2026). Valkey states that it is compatible with Redis OSS 7.2 and all earlier open-source Redis versions, that moving from one of them is an upgrade, that existing Redis client libraries connect without code changes, and that data files of Redis Community Edition 7.4 and later are not compatible with it (Valkey migration page); that statement is not made for a later Redis release, for Redis modules or for features Redis added after 7.2. A Valkey source is the same product. STACKIT offers Valkey 8 (service catalogue of eu01), so an engine version other than 8 is an upgrade and says so in its own finding. Do not target stackit_redis_instance: it is deprecated and removed after August 2027.
- **Deployment prerequisites:** passed StackToIT's own draft checks; tofu validate: PASSED; NOT deployment-ready: 3 open decisions, 0 manual actions
  - decision `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-DATA-MOVEMENT/module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default` (alias `convert:module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default:data:DATA_MOVEMENT`): Choose how the keys of module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default move from aws_elasticache_replication_group to STACKIT Key Value Store (stackit_valkey_instance.dependencies_checkout_elasticache_redis_default): the keys copied by a client inside the write freeze, or a tool of your own · the plan defines the one method the STACKIT pages and the ElastiCache pages allow together, a copy of the keys by a client (SCAN, DUMP, PTTL, RESTORE) inside the write freeze, and the customer's own tool; loading an exported snapshot is refused because no STACKIT page or API takes an RDB file, and following the source as a replica is refused because neither end offers it; the source does not say whether the cache holds only what the application rebuilds, the size of the data, the downtime the business accepts or the shards the keys are spread over, and the Key Value Store has no sharding; the steps wait for the answer
  - decision `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-SIZE-FROM-SOURCE/module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default` (alias `convert:module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default:elasticache_redis_plan_name:SIZE_FROM_SOURCE`): Confirm the plan the draft derived for module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default from the size the source states: option use-the-size-derived-from-the-source (stackit-keyvalue-1.4.10-single, in var.elasticache_redis_plan_name) or option choose-the-size (a plan you set in var.elasticache_redis_plan_name) · the target sizes by a plan of the catalogue, and the source states a size in the terms of its own offer; the draft proposes the smallest plan that covers it, which leaves out what the source does not state (the disk of a plan, the sustained load, the headroom of a pool), so the value is a proposal and the price of the draft moves with it; unit of the memory figure: the plan table gives its RAM as "GB" and does not say whether a GB is 10^9 or 2^30 bytes, so the plan is chosen to hold the source's memory when a GB is 10^9 bytes, which it then also does when a GB is 2^30 bytes (it is never smaller than the source whichever way the table is read)

- **WARNING** (redis-transit-encryption-off): The source cache runs without TLS (transit_encryption_enabled = false). STACKIT Key Value Store accepts TLS only: a test on a STACKIT project (Valkey 8, single plan, eu01, 2026-09-24) found a plaintext connection closed by the server, TLS 1.2 and 1.3 accepted, and the how-to connects with rediss:// and valkey-cli --tls. Every client of the cache changes: the scheme redis:// becomes rediss://, the driver's TLS option is switched on and the instance's CA certificate is trusted, before the cutover.

Moving the data upgrades the engine: test the application and the data against the version you choose before the cutover; the draft does not choose it.

#### `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_kms_key.cmk`

- **Source:** `aws_kms_key` — ../../lib/ecs/common.tf:1
- **Module:** `module.retail_app_ecs` (../../lib/ecs)
- **Candidate target:** STACKIT KMS (`stackit_kms_keyring`, `stackit_kms_key`)
- **Target maturity:** GA · lifecycle active · region availability not established by a catalogue snapshot
- **Classification:** RECONFIGURE · **Confidence:** MEDIUM · rule `aws.kms_key/kms`
- **Feasibility:** CONDITIONAL — blocked by identity or access mapping (semantic deletion-window); a point the scan could not establish
- **Why:** Keys are re-created in STACKIT KMS (keyring + key); key material generated in AWS KMS cannot be exported, so encrypted data is re-encrypted during migration. Consumers (volumes, PostgreSQL Flex, Secrets Manager) reference the new key.
- **INFO** (kms-deletion-window): The key's deletion window is not 30 days. STACKIT KMS deletes a key scheduled for deletion after 30 days, with no setting (stackit_kms_key has none) — check that a different recovery window is acceptable.
- **WARNING** (source-provider-unpinned): No lock file entry pins aws (>= 3.0, >= 3.29, >= 4.18, >= 4.30, >= 4.59, >= 5.64, >= 5.79, ~> 5.0): the version the plan would use is not proven, so the knowledge base (checked against 5.100.0) is not taken as proof of it (stated once for the whole estate, with the units it covers and the rest of the words)
- **Critical semantics left to you to decide or accept (1):** deletion-window
- **Property accounting:** 1 of 1 meaningful properties accounted
- **1 critical setting(s) left to you** — **not automation-eligible**:
  - deletion protection (1; REVIEW 1): `deletion_window_in_days` — NOT CARRIED (module.retail_app_ecs.aws_kms_key.cmk): deletion_window_in_days = 7 — STACKIT KMS deletes a key scheduled for deletion after 30 days; stackit_kms_key has no deletion window (pinned provider).
- **Why those are open, and what closes them:**
  - `deletion-window`: A check of the knowledge base (kms deletion window) fired on this unit and leaves the setting to a person: the target does not settle it by itself (a finding of this unit describes it). STACKIT KMS deletes a key scheduled for deletion after 30 days (docs create-and-manage-keys, 'Delete a key'), with no setting: 30 is preserved, another window review. Closes when: A person decides what this finding asks, and records the evidence or the decision. The finding says: The key's deletion window is not 30 days. STACKIT KMS deletes a key scheduled for deletion after 30 days, with no setting (stackit_kms_key has none) — check that a different recovery window is acceptable.
- **Evidence** (validated against terraform-provider-stackit@0.116.0; docs security/kms 2026-09-23 (eu01, eu02 since 2026-04-07); docs security/kms create-and-manage-keys 2026-09-27 (deleted after 30 days, active after creation); found compatible through 0.121.0 by the release check): https://docs.stackit.cloud/products/security/kms/ · https://registry.terraform.io/providers/stackitcloud/stackit/0.116.0/docs/resources/kms_key

- **GA target:** no GA target yet: stackit_authorization_project_role_assignment is experimental (feature iam) in the pinned STACKIT provider (a blocker the provider's own maturity notice raises; the class and the confidence are unchanged).
- **Classification:** RECONFIGURE · **Confidence:** MEDIUM · rule `aws.iam_role/role-workload-execution`
- **Feasibility:** CONDITIONAL — blocked by identity or access mapping (semantic trust, semantic permissions, semantic tcfp-iam-role-policy-attachment--policy-arn, semantic iam-trust-service-workload and 1 more); a point the scan could not establish
- **Why:** This draft writes no stackit_service_account, stackit_service_account_key, stackit_authorization_project_role_assignment for this unit: the role of the ECS platform agent has no service account counterpart: its jobs are steps. The knowledge base maps the unit to STACKIT service account for the workload (stackit_service_account, stackit_service_account_key, stackit_authorization_project_role_assignment); this draft writes no block of it, so it creates nothing for the unit on STACKIT.
- **Includes:** `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_iam_role_policy_attachment.task_execution_role_policy`
- **INFO** (iam-trust-service-workload): Trust policy: lambda.amazonaws.com or ecs-tasks.amazonaws.com assumes this role, so an application workload (a Lambda function or an ECS task) runs as it. On STACKIT the workload runs as a service account (stackit_service_account) and nothing assumes it: it authenticates with a service account key (stackit_service_account_key, key flow) or, on SKE, by Workload Identity Federation (a federated identity provider on the account). What to decide: key or federation, and the roles of the attached policies (see the permission findings of this unit).
- **INFO** (iam-managed-ecs): Managed policy of an ECS role (AWS: task execution pulls images from ECR and writes CloudWatch Logs; the container-instance role registers hosts with ECS). STACKIT has no ECS: image pull is a registry credential (Container Registry roles, Guest is read-only) and log shipping a platform or credential setting (stackit_logs_access_token); no STACKIT role replaces these grants. Container Registry: STACKIT offers this service. A public STACKIT API exists for it. The pinned Terraform provider has no resource for it. StackToIT drafts no Terraform for it and no STACKIT API step for this grant; extra implementation is needed. (schema checked 2026-10-11: none of the 115 resource types of the pinned STACKIT provider), so the pull credential is not drafted (project roles: docs.stackit.cloud, updated Jul 2, 2026). What to decide: the registry pull credential and the log destination of the replacing workload.

- **INFO** (iam-trust-service-workload): Trust policy: lambda.amazonaws.com or ecs-tasks.amazonaws.com assumes this role, so an application workload (a Lambda function or an ECS task) runs as it. On STACKIT the workload runs as a service account (stackit_service_account) and nothing assumes it: it authenticates with a service account key (stackit_service_account_key, key flow) or, on SKE, by Workload Identity Federation (a federated identity provider on the account). What to decide: key or federation, and the roles of the attached policies (see the permission findings of this unit).
- **INFO** (iam-managed-ecs): Managed policy of an ECS role (AWS: task execution pulls images from ECR and writes CloudWatch Logs; the container-instance role registers hosts with ECS). STACKIT has no ECS: image pull is a registry credential (Container Registry roles, Guest is read-only) and log shipping a platform or credential setting (stackit_logs_access_token); no STACKIT role replaces these grants. Container Registry: STACKIT offers this service. A public STACKIT API exists for it. The pinned Terraform provider has no resource for it. StackToIT drafts no Terraform for it and no STACKIT API step for this grant; extra implementation is needed. (schema checked 2026-10-11: none of the 115 resource types of the pinned STACKIT provider), so the pull credential is not drafted (project roles: docs.stackit.cloud, updated Jul 2, 2026). What to decide: the registry pull credential and the log destination of the replacing workload.
- **INFO** (iam-inline-kms): A policy of this role grants KMS actions. STACKIT KMS access comes from role assignments on an organisation, folder, project or service account, not on a key ring or key (stackit_kms_key has no policy or grant attribute in the pinned STACKIT provider), so a per-key permission widens to every key of the project. What to decide: which identities may use and which may administer keys, and whether keys that need different access move to their own project.
- **INFO** (iam-inline-secrets): A policy of this role reads or writes secrets or parameters. STACKIT Secrets Manager grants access with role bindings on the instance and on secret groups (stackit_secretsmanager_instance_role_binding_v1, stackit_secretsmanager_secret_group_role_binding_v1; documented roles Secrets Manager Reader and Admin) and with users that carry credentials. What to decide: per secret group, which identity reads and which writes. One more limit exists per instance: a source-address list (stackit_secretsmanager_instance acls, IP addresses or CIDR ranges that may reach the instance), the nearest thing to an aws:SourceIp condition; there is no per-identity or per-secret condition.

### Messaging

#### `module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq`

- **Source:** `aws_mq_broker` — ../../lib/dependencies/mq.tf:12
- **Module:** `module.dependencies` (../../lib/dependencies)
- **Candidate target:** STACKIT RabbitMQ (`stackit_rabbitmq_instance`, `stackit_rabbitmq_credential`)
- **Target maturity:** GA · lifecycle active · offered in eu01, eu02 (live catalogue snapshot)
- **Classification:** RECONFIGURE · **Confidence:** MEDIUM · rule `aws.mq_broker/rabbitmq`
- **Feasibility:** CONDITIONAL — blocked by a feature the draft does not carry yet (check mq-rabbitmq-major-version, check engine-version-upgrade, semantic engine-version, semantic broker-network and 10 more); a point the scan could not establish
- **Why:** The product is the same (RabbitMQ), the release is not, and the move is not like-for-like. The topology (virtual hosts, exchanges, queues, bindings and policies) is exported from the Amazon MQ broker as RabbitMQ definitions and imported into STACKIT RabbitMQ; the messages are not part of definitions, the draft moves no data, and the broker users are not carried (definitions hold password hashes; STACKIT generates the credentials), so clients change host and credentials. Versions: Amazon MQ offers RabbitMQ 3.13 and 4.2 or 4.3 (AWS documentation); the eu01 catalogue lists 4.1 (read 2026-10-18) while STACKIT's lifecycle page (last updated 2026-03-11, read again 2026-11-02) lists 4.0 and 3.13 and no 4.1, so the version the instance accepts is confirmed before the cutover. A source on 3.13 crosses a major version: classic queue mirroring (the ha-mode policies) was removed in 4.0, so a mirrored classic queue becomes a quorum queue, and the definitions are changed before the import to drop the mirroring keys of the policies (RabbitMQ migration guides). What else changes with a quorum queue (lazy mode, queue priorities, reject-publish-dlx, exclusive queues) is checked per queue and in the application. A source on 4.2 or 4.3 is newer than either version STACKIT names; whether its definitions import is not stated by any page read. Nothing here was run: the import of definitions across these versions was not tried against a STACKIT instance, and this text states what the documents say. The confidence is MEDIUM whatever the version the source states: the RabbitMQ page on definitions states no compatibility between releases for an import, a source on 4.2 or 4.3 is newer than any version STACKIT names, a source on 3.13 crosses the major version at which mirroring was removed, and a version the scan cannot read is a finding that is not verifiable statically and decides nothing; whether the topology imports is known only after the import has been run.

#### `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_vpc.this`

- **Source:** `aws_vpc` — .terraform/modules/vpc.vpc/main.tf:28
- **Module:** `module.vpc.module.vpc` (terraform-aws-modules/vpc/aws 5.21.0)
- **Candidate target:** STACKIT project networking / Network Area (SNA) (`stackit_network_area`, `stackit_network`)
- **Target maturity:** GA · lifecycle active · region availability not established by a catalogue snapshot
- **Classification:** RECONFIGURE · **Confidence:** MEDIUM · rule `aws.vpc/vpc`
- **Feasibility:** CONDITIONAL — blocked by network reachability, restrictions or public exposure (check vpc-main-table-undecided, semantic default-route-table, semantic main-table-set, check vpc-default-sg-no-egress-rules and 17 more); a point the scan could not establish
- **Why:** STACKIT has no VPC container object in its GA model: each subnet becomes a stackit_network in a project, and networks that must talk to each other privately join a Network Area. The newer stackit_vpc resources exist but are experimental (provider experiment "vpc"). With exactly one VPC in the estate the project is that VPC's boundary (STACKIT_STABLE profile) and the draft says so at the top of the draft's network file; with several VPCs, or a VPC count that is not visible statically, which architecture holds them is the architect's decision (the checks on several VPCs and on a VPC count that is not visible).
- **Includes:** `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_default_network_acl.this`, `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_default_route_table.default`, `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_default_security_group.this`
- **WARNING** (vpc-main-table-undecided): The scan cannot say which route table is the main route table of this VPC: the VPC has count or for_each (or may be absent), an aws_main_route_table_association may name it through a value the scan cannot resolve (a variable, a literal ID, a conditional, a value from a module call), two resources set it (two main associations, or a main association and an aws_default_route_table on one VPC, which the provider documents as conflicting), or the table is a variable or lies outside the configuration. node.vpc_main_table carries the cause. Which table the VPC's subnets follow without an association of their own, and so what their routing and exposure are, is not decided here; the draft attaches no routing table through the main table.
- **INFO** (vpc-default-sg-no-ingress-rules): The adopted default security group states no ingress rule. The AWS provider removes every rule of the default group when it adopts it and creates only the stated ones, so after apply the group allows no inbound traffic at all, not even from the members of the group itself. A new STACKIT security group allows no incoming traffic either, not even between its own members: they reach each other only through a rule whose remote_security_group_id is the group (tested on a STACKIT project on 2026-10-08, IPv4; the concepts page sentence that traffic from the same group is allowed holds for the project's default group, which carries explicit rules for its own members, not for a group you create), so in the ingress direction an empty STACKIT group equals the adopted group. IPv6 between members is not established (no IPv6 network could be created in the probed project). Nothing is drafted for the adopted group: decide which servers relied on the default group and attach them to a STACKIT group that states no ingress rule, not to the project's default group, whose own-member rules would admit its members.
- **WARNING** (vpc-default-sg-no-egress-rules): The adopted default security group states no egress rule. The AWS provider removes every rule of the default group when it adopts it and creates only the stated ones, so after apply the group allows no outbound traffic at all (the provider page calls this denying all egress by omitting the rules). A new STACKIT security group allows all outgoing traffic, so a server that moves onto a STACKIT group is wider than the source unless the allow-all egress rules are deleted after apply.
- **WARNING** (source-provider-unpinned): No lock file entry pins aws (>= 3.0, >= 3.29, >= 4.18, >= 4.30, >= 4.59, >= 5.64, >= 5.79, ~> 5.0): the version the plan would use is not proven, so the knowledge base (checked against 5.100.0) is not taken as proof of it (stated once for the whole estate, with the units it covers and the rest of the words)
- **Critical semantics left to you to decide or accept (5):** dns-settings, default-nacl-rules, default-route-table, default-security-group-omitted-direction, main-table-set (**not automation-eligible**: no property the unit sets is read by default-security-group-omitted-direction, main-table-set)
- **17 critical setting(s) left to you** (implicit unit) — **not automation-eligible**:

#### `module.retail_app_ecs.module.alb.aws_security_group.this`

- Alike lines: see "What the 12 aws_security_group units state alike", above.
- **Source:** `aws_security_group` — .terraform/modules/retail_app_ecs.alb/main.tf:807
- **Module:** `module.retail_app_ecs.module.alb` (terraform-aws-modules/alb/aws 8.7.0)
- **Candidate target:** STACKIT security group (`stackit_security_group`, `stackit_security_group_rule`)
- **Target maturity:** GA · lifecycle active · region availability not established by a catalogue snapshot
- **Feasibility:** CONDITIONAL — blocked by network reachability, restrictions or public exposure (check sg-egress-restricted, semantic security-group-vpc-membership, semantic stateful, semantic egress-restriction); a point the scan could not establish
- **Why:** STACKIT security groups are stateful rule sets like AWS's, including rules that reference another security group (remote_security_group_id). Rules are re-created one by one; CIDRs of AWS-internal ranges (VPC, prefix lists) must be replaced with STACKIT network prefixes.
- **Behaviour evidence:** behaviour: 2 observed on target, 4 documented, 2 not verified for this variant, 0 contradicted
- **Depends on:** `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_vpc.this`
- **WARNING** (sg-egress-restricted): The source restricts egress (the AWS provider removes AWS's default allow-all egress rule; only the listed egress rules apply). Every new STACKIT security group gets two default egress rules, IPv4 and IPv6, with no ip range (any address), no protocol and no port restriction (the test of 2026-10-07, 2026-10-07: API read-back of a new group, docs concepts page), that stackit_security_group (the pinned STACKIT provider) does not manage — as generated, the draft allows all egress. After apply, delete them (stackit security-group rule list / delete --security-group-id <id>), or egress is wider than in the source.
- **Critical semantics left to you to decide or accept (3):** egress-restriction, security-group-vpc-membership, stateful (**not automation-eligible**: no property the unit sets is read by egress-restriction, stateful)
- **Property accounting:** 1 of 1 meaningful properties accounted
- **1 critical setting(s) left to you** — **not automation-eligible**:
  - network exposure (1; REVIEW 1): `vpc_id`
- **Why those are open, and what closes them:**
  - `security-group-vpc-membership`: Not enforced: the security group is attached to no block of the target (it is written in the draft and listed by no resource, or, where the access lists of its members carry it, not written), so its rules ("security group rules") restrict nothing on the target until an attachment is designed; this setting is not carried as an enforced control. Unsupported, by name: a STACKIT security group has no network (VPC) scope. stackit_security_group takes project_id, name, description, labels, stateful and region only (pinned provider), and the IaaS API object SecurityGroup has no network field either (stackit-api-specifications services/iaas/v2); the group is project-wide and can be attached to the network interfaces of every network of the project. #### `module.dependencies.aws_security_group.catalog_opensearch_sg`

- Alike lines: see "What the 12 aws_security_group units state alike", above.
- **Source:** `aws_security_group` — ../../lib/dependencies/catalog_opensearch.tf:14
- **Module:** `module.dependencies` (../../lib/dependencies)
- **Candidate target:** not written by this draft (stackit_security_group, stackit_security_group_rule)
- **Feasibility:** CONDITIONAL — blocked by network reachability, restrictions or public exposure (check sg-egress-restricted, check sg-rule-not-rendered, semantic security-group-vpc-membership, semantic stateful and 5 more); a point the scan could not establish
- **Why:** This draft writes no stackit_security_group, stackit_security_group_rule for this unit: every unit that names the group is a managed data service of STACKIT, which has no security group and takes its clients from an access list of its own, so the group is listed by no resource and what it admitted reaches those access lists as addresses only. The knowledge base maps the unit to STACKIT security group (stackit_security_group, stackit_security_group_rule); this draft writes no block of it, so it creates nothing for the unit on STACKIT.
- **Includes:** `module.dependencies.aws_security_group_rule.allow_https_from_client`
- **Depends on:** `module.retail_app_ecs.module.catalog_service.aws_security_group.this`, `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_vpc.this`
- **WARNING** (sg-egress-restricted): The source restricts egress (the AWS provider removes AWS's default allow-all egress rule; only the listed egress rules apply). Every new STACKIT security group gets two default egress rules, IPv4 and IPv6, with no ip range (any address), no protocol and no port restriction (the test of 2026-10-07, 2026-10-07: API read-back of a new group, docs concepts page), that stackit_security_group (the pinned STACKIT provider) does not manage — as generated, the draft allows all egress. After apply, delete them (stackit security-group rule list / delete --security-group-id <id>), or egress is wider than in the source.
- **WARNING** (sg-rule-not-rendered): At least one rule of this group is not in the draft as the source has it. The plan withholds it, holds it as a commented-out rule until a value or a decision is filled in, writes it with a fact that decides what it allows not carried exactly, or cannot name it at all because the source has more rules than are visible statically (a dynamic block over a collection, a count or a rule list that is not known until apply, or a list with a hidden member: the plan's INCOMPLETE action says which set and why, the draft's TODO "rules beyond the known set" names it, and only the known rules are drafted). A tcp or udp range that starts at port 0 and ends below 65535 is written from port 1 (the STACKIT API rejects a range that starts at 0; tested on a STACKIT project on 2026-10-07): port 0 itself is not carried. A stackit_security_group_rule without protocol, port_range or source allows everything (the pinned STACKIT provider), so such a rule is never widened; re-create it with the values it needs (the plan names each one).

#### `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_nat_gateway.this`

- **Source:** `aws_nat_gateway` — .terraform/modules/vpc.vpc/main.tf:1111
- **Module:** `module.vpc.module.vpc` (terraform-aws-modules/vpc/aws 5.21.0)
- **Candidate target:** implicit outbound (STACKIT network)
- **Classification:** RECONFIGURE · **Confidence:** MEDIUM · rule `aws.nat_gateway/nat`
- **Feasibility:** CONDITIONAL — blocked by network reachability, restrictions or public exposure (semantic fixed-egress-ip, semantic nat-placement); a point the scan could not establish
- **Why:** STACKIT has no discrete NAT gateway resource. Outbound traffic from private workloads leaves through the network's default routing (observed on the target, the test of 2026-10-10, in a project outside a Network Area), by the network's publicIp - one address shared by every routed network of the project.
- **Depends on:** `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_eip.nat`, `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_internet_gateway.this`, `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_subnet.public`
- **WARNING** (source-provider-unpinned): No lock file entry pins aws (>= 3.0, >= 3.29, >= 4.18, >= 4.30, >= 4.59, >= 5.64, >= 5.79, ~> 5.0): the version the plan would use is not proven, so the knowledge base (checked against 5.100.0) is not taken as proof of it (stated once for the whole estate, with the units it covers and the rest of the words)
- **Critical semantics left to you to decide or accept (2):** fixed-egress-ip, nat-placement
- **2 critical setting(s) left to you** (implicit unit) — **not automation-eligible**:
  - network exposure (2; REVIEW 2): `allocation_id`, `subnet_id`
- **Why those are open, and what closes them:**
  - `fixed-egress-ip`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. A stable egress IP on STACKIT is an open question (no source in this repository answers it; the per-network SNAT address is computed and cannot be chosen or kept). Closes when: A person decides how peers that allow-list the Elastic IP are served: a routed STACKIT network leaves by the project's shared egress address, which cannot be set and can change, so the allow-list follows it or each server gets a STACKIT public IP.
  - `nat-placement`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. A zonal NAT gateway (subnet_id) is placed in one AZ; a regional one lists its per-AZ egress in availability_zone_address(es).availability_zone. STACKIT has no NAT gateway resource and one outbound path serves every zone, so a per-zone egress has no counterpart, as for the zones of an Azure NAT gateway. Closes when: A person decides whether one egress path for every zone is acceptable where the source keeps one NAT gateway per zone (for zone independence or per-zone addresses), or accepts the difference; a source with one zonal NAT gateway for every subnet loses nothing in placement.
- **Review:**
  - [ ] If partners allowlist the fixed egress IP of the NAT gateway, that address is lost (observed, tested on a STACKIT project on 2026-10-10: the network's publicIp is shared by every routed network of the project, changed twice when every network of the project was deleted, and cannot be set - a PATCH answers 202 and is ignored). A STACKIT public IP per server keeps its address (the test of 2026-10-10); otherwise the allow-list has to follow the changing address.
- **Evidence** (validated against terraform-provider-stackit@0.116.0 (no NAT-gateway resource); terraform-provider-stackit@0.120.0 (re-checked 2026-10-18 against STACKIT provider schema 0.120.0 and the v0.120.0 tag docs: no such resource in the 0.120.0 schema (115 resources; since 0.116.0 only stackit_volume_automation was added)); found compatible through 0.121.0 by the release check): https://docs.stackit.cloud/products/network/core-networking/basics/architecture/

#### `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private`

- **Source:** `aws_route_table` — .terraform/modules/vpc.vpc/main.tf:296 · instances: 0–1 instances (decided by `module.vpc.data.aws_availability_zones.available`); POSSIBLE_SOURCE_ERROR: `slice(…, 3)` fails in Terraform and OpenTofu when the list has fewer than 3 elements (the source proves none), so the count is not exact
- **Standing:** conditional — whether it exists is decided only when a plan runs; the class below is the one it would get, it is not counted in the totals and not automation-eligible.
- **Configuration:** conditional — whether the unit exists is decided when a plan runs, and so is part of what configures it; the facets below are open too.
  - the configuration of `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table_association.private` is decided by `module.vpc.data.aws_availability_zones.available` (a data source, read when a plan runs)
- **Module:** `module.vpc.module.vpc` (terraform-aws-modules/vpc/aws 5.21.0)
- **Candidate target:** implicit (STACKIT network default routing)
- **Classification:** RECONFIGURE · **Confidence:** LOW · rule `aws.route_table/rt-default-only-nat`
- **Feasibility:** CONDITIONAL — blocked by network reachability, restrictions or public exposure (check topology-route-table-not-drafted, check rt-private-subnet-association, semantic subnet-association-target, semantic tcr-route-table-vpc-id and 6 more); a point the scan could not establish
- **Why:** A route of the table sends traffic to a NAT gateway (the others, if any, to an internet gateway), and the draft's project is outside a Network Area. Observed on the target (tested on a STACKIT project in eu01 on 2026-10-10, a project outside a Network Area): a routed STACKIT network gives servers with and without a public IP the same outbound reachability with no route and no routing table, nothing initiated from the internet reaches a server behind the network address, and egress is restricted by the egress rules of security groups. The table is therefore not drafted: STACKIT's default routing replaces it. What a NAT gateway has beyond an internet route is NOT CARRIED and stays an open review: the gateway's fixed egress address is LOST (observed: the address a routed network leaves by is its project's publicIp, one address for every routed network of the project, not fixed - it changed after every network of the project was deleted, twice - and not settable, a PATCH answers 202 and is ignored), so a third party's allow-list on the NAT gateway's address does not survive and needs a STACKIT public IP per server (the only address the probe saw stay fixed); the restriction of egress to the gateway is carried only by security-group egress rules, which the draft does not tie to this table; the absence of an inbound path is observed. LOW: the loss is for a person to decide, as the plan decision NAT_FIXED_EGRESS_ADDRESS_LOST. The egress itself is not widened where the gateway is a public one: the subnets of this table reached the internet through it in the source, so the plan decision EXPOSURE_WIDENED_EGRESS (a subnet whose source had no route to the internet; a subnet behind a private NAT gateway is one, a private gateway gives no internet access) is not what this table raises; what changes is the address. STACKIT documents the way out as source NAT of the network (Virtual Network concepts, last updated Mar 11, 2026). Which NAT gateway the route names does not change this: a table that picks its NAT gateway by an index (one gateway per availability zone) or by a conditional between NAT gateways is decided as a table with one plain reference, because every gateway it can name is lost the same way and STACKIT has no NAT gateway to pick. A Gateway endpoint of S3 or DynamoDB that names the table adds a route that is NOT CARRIED (Object Storage is reached over the network's default path); it does not change the case, a check on the gateway endpoint names it. The destinations are read by the check of the route destinations. (1 conditional relation could not be resolved statically because module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table_association.private's count/for_each is not known statically (it may not exist); the mapping decides the same either way; affected: this unit and `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table_association.private`.)
- **Includes:** `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route.private_nat_gateway`, `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table_association.private`
- **Depends on:** `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_nat_gateway.this`, `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_subnet.private`, `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_vpc.this`
- **WARNING** (topology-route-table-not-drafted): This route table is created with count or for_each. What the draft makes of it follows the decision of this unit. A table that STACKIT's default routing replaces (the cases of a table with only internet or NAT routes in a project outside a Network Area) is not drafted at all, whatever its count: no routing table is made and none is to be attached, and this warning only records that the table is repeated. A table the decision keeps as a routing table of its own (rt-custom) is made only where the source names, instance by instance, which route and which subnet belongs to which instance (a table created with count whose routes and associations name the table by their own count.index): one routing table for each instance, with the routes the draft carries in each, attached to no network, and none at all when the draft carries none of the routes. Where the source does not name them, the draft makes none (which instance a route belongs to is not decided). For a table the decision keeps, nothing in the draft states which VPC the table belonged to (the routing table the draft writes, stackit_routing_table, belongs to a Network Area; the experimental stackit_vpc_routing_table of the pinned provider takes a vpc_id, but the draft writes none): the table, its routes and the subnets it is associated with are re-created or attached by hand once the VPC architecture is decided.
- **WARNING** (rt-private-subnet-association): A subnet uses a route table whose default route goes through a NAT gateway — a private subnet in AWS. STACKIT networks have no public/private split: a server there is reachable as soon as it gets a public IP, only its security group restricts it. Keep the converted servers without a public IP (or restrict them) where privacy is needed.
- **WARNING** (source-provider-unpinned): No lock file entry pins aws (>= 3.0, >= 3.29, >= 4.18, >= 4.30, >= 4.59, >= 5.64, >= 5.79, ~> 5.0): the version the plan would use is not proven, so the knowledge base (checked against 5.100.0) is not taken as proof of it (stated once for the whole estate, with the units it covers and the rest of the words)
- **Critical semantics left to you to decide or accept (7):** routes, route-destinations, route-targets, nat-fixed-egress-address, egress-restriction, subnet-association-target, tcr-route-table-vpc-id (**not automation-eligible**: no property the unit sets is read by egress-restriction, nat-fixed-egress-address)
  - not read instance by instance (unknown_count: the number of instances is not known statically): `routes`, `route-destinations`, `route-targets`, `nat-fixed-egress-address`, `egress-restriction`, `subnet-association-target`, `tcr-route-table-vpc-id`
- **4 critical setting(s) left to you** (implicit unit) — **not automation-eligible**:
  - network exposure (4; REVIEW 4): `vpc_id`, `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route.private_nat_gateway.destination_cidr_block`, `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route.private_nat_gateway.nat_gateway_id`, `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table_association.private.subnet_id`
- **Why those are open, and what closes them:**
  - `routes`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. Custom routes map to stackit_routing_table_route, an experimental resource (experiment routing-tables); next hops that name AWS gateways are chosen anew. Closes when: A person creates the routes on the STACKIT routing table (stackit_routing_table_route, experimental) and chooses the next hops anew, because they name AWS gateways.
  - `route-destinations`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. Destinations are read by value: every route of a default-only table sends 0.0.0.0/0 to an internet or NAT gateway — STACKIT's default network routing, observed equivalent for a draft outside a Network Area (the test of 2026-10-10) and review all the same. Closes when: A person confirms every route destination that is not the default route to an internet or NAT gateway (a CIDR, a prefix list, any IPv6 route): the draft carries none, and a specific destination needs a stackit_routing_table_route, which takes IPv4 CIDR destinations only.
  - `route-targets`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The next hop of each route of the table: a route whose target is anything but an internet or NAT gateway, or that names none, makes the table a custom routing table. Closes when: A person decides the next hop of each route on STACKIT: an internet gateway becomes the next hop internet, an appliance (a network interface or an instance) a next hop of type ipv4. A NAT gateway, a transit gateway or a VPC peering has no STACKIT counterpart, so the connectivity design is the person's.
  - `nat-fixed-egress-address`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. NOT CARRIED, an observed loss (the test of 2026-10-10): the NAT gateway's fixed egress address. Closes when: A person accepts the project's shared egress address, which can change and cannot be set, or gives each server a STACKIT public IP when a third party allow-lists the old address.
  - `egress-restriction`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The restriction of egress to the NAT gateway (the only way out of a private subnet) is carried on STACKIT by the egress rules of the security groups, not by a route (the test of 2026-10-10: with egress rules for one /32 only that destination was reachable, with none nothing left). Whether the source restricts egress that way, and whether the draft carries those rules for the servers of this table's subnets, is not something this contract can read, so it is left for review and never preserved: a person confirms that the servers of the private subnets keep a restricting egress rule set. Closes when: A person confirms that the servers of the private subnets keep a restricting egress rule set in their security groups, because on STACKIT the restriction of egress is carried by egress rules, not by a route.
  - `subnet-association-target`: A check of the knowledge base (rt private subnet association) fired on this unit and leaves the setting to a person: the target does not settle it by itself (a finding of this unit describes it). The setting states which subnet uses this route table, for the associations the draft does not attach. Closes when: A person decides it as that finding asks, and the finding's closing evidence or decision is recorded. A person decides which networks of the Network Area take the routing table and sets routing_table_id on them: STACKIT attaches a routing table to a whole network (stackit_network.routing_table_id), there is no per-subnet association, and routing tables apply only to routed Layer 3 networks in a Network Area.
  - and 1 kind of reason more (engineering/analysis-details.md)

#### `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.public`

- **Source:** `aws_route_table` — .terraform/modules/vpc.vpc/main.tf:164 · instances: 0–1 instances (decided by `module.vpc.data.aws_availability_zones.available`); POSSIBLE_SOURCE_ERROR: `slice(…, 3)` fails in Terraform and OpenTofu when the list has fewer than 3 elements (the source proves none), so the count is not exact
- **Standing:** conditional — whether it exists is decided only when a plan runs; the class below is the one it would get, it is not counted in the totals and not automation-eligible.
- **Configuration:** conditional — whether the unit exists is decided when a plan runs, and so is part of what configures it; the facets below are open too.
  - the configuration of `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route.public_internet_gateway` is decided by `module.vpc.data.aws_availability_zones.available` (a data source, read when a plan runs)
  - the configuration of `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table_association.public` is decided by `module.vpc.data.aws_availability_zones.available` (a data source, read when a plan runs)
- **Module:** `module.vpc.module.vpc` (terraform-aws-modules/vpc/aws 5.21.0)
- **Candidate target:** implicit (STACKIT network default routing)
- **Classification:** RECONFIGURE · **Confidence:** MEDIUM · rule `aws.route_table/rt-default-only`
- **Feasibility:** CONDITIONAL — blocked by network reachability, restrictions or public exposure (check topology-route-table-not-drafted, semantic tcr-route-table-vpc-id, semantic routes, semantic route-destinations and 1 more); a point the scan could not establish
- **Why:** Every route of the table sends traffic to an internet gateway (a reference to an aws_internet_gateway, or an ID that is not a virtual private gateway's) and the draft's project is outside a Network Area. Observed on the target (tested on a STACKIT project in eu01 on 2026-10-10, a project outside a Network Area): a routed STACKIT network gives servers with and without a public IP the same outbound reachability with no route and no routing table configured (HTTPS, ICMP, DNS and TCP 443 in 62 of 62 rounds over 30 minutes per server; a non-routed network reaches nothing), nothing initiated from the internet reaches a server behind the network address (tcp 22, 80, 8080 timed out in 137 of 137 samples with an admitting security group), egress is restricted by the egress rules of security groups, and routed networks reach each other with no table. The table is therefore not drafted: STACKIT's default routing replaces it. Equivalent for a server that has a public IP. NOT equivalent for a server WITHOUT one: in the source an internet gateway serves only an instance with a public IPv4 or Elastic IP address ("the instances in the public subnet must have public IP addresses or Elastic IP addresses to enable communication with the internet over the internet gateway", AWS internet gateway documentation), so such an instance has no internet path; on STACKIT the same server leaves through the SNAT address of its network (observed in the STACKIT sandbox on two rounds; "VM without one: the SNAT address of its network, shared by every VM in that network without its own public IP", stackitcloud/professional-service examples/vpn-full-tunnel-central-egress README). The draft therefore GIVES egress to servers the source did not give it; the table does not carry that, and it is decided on the subnet (the finding that the egress of the subnet is not proven, and the plan decision EXPOSURE_WIDENED_EGRESS of the subnet, private egress ipv4), not here. What the STACKIT documentation says of the same behaviour (Virtual Network concepts, last updated Mar 11, 2026): "Every subnet is routed to the public internet. Technically this is done by source NAT (SNAT) in combination with masquerading", it "supports outbound internet access but does not allow traffic initiated from the public internet to reach your resources", and the routing "can be controlled using Routing tables" only for projects that are part of a Network Area. So outside an area the equivalence rests on the documentation and on the test of 2026-10-10, which agree. Not HIGH: the test was one project, one region, one day, no probe in a Network Area (a draft that writes a Network Area is the case of a table in a Network Area), and the servers without a public IP are not equivalent (above). The destinations are read by the check of the route destinations: only 0.0.0.0/0 is default routing, any other destination (IPv6 included) is left for a person to decide. A Gateway endpoint of S3 or DynamoDB that names the table adds a route that is NOT CARRIED and does not change the case. (2 conditional relations could not be resolved statically because module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route.public_internet_gateway's count/for_each is not known statically (it may not exist); module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table_association.public's count/for_each is not known statically (it may not exist); the mapping decides the same either way; affected: this unit and `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route.public_internet_gateway`, `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table_association.public`.)

Closes when: A person confirms every route destination that is not the default route to an internet or NAT gateway (a CIDR, a prefix list, any IPv6 route): the draft carries none, and a specific destination needs a stackit_routing_table_route, which takes IPv4 CIDR destinations only.
  - `route-targets`: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The next hop of each route of the table: a route whose target is anything but an internet or NAT gateway, or that names none, makes the table a custom routing table. Closes when: A person decides the next hop of each route on STACKIT: an internet gateway becomes the next hop internet, an appliance (a network interface or an instance) a next hop of type ipv4. A NAT gateway, a transit gateway or a VPC peering has no STACKIT counterpart, so the connectivity design is the person's.
  - `tcr-route-table-vpc-id`: A check of the knowledge base (topology route table not drafted) fired on this unit and leaves the setting to a person: the target does not settle it by itself (a finding of this unit describes it). aws_route_table.vpc_id: The VPC whose subnets this route table's rules apply to; a route table's entries only make sense, and only affect reachability, within this one VPC, so losing or mismapping it silently changes which network the routing rules govern. A custom table is left for review whatever the estate: the draft's stackit_routing_table is organisation and Network Area scoped (organization_id, network_area_id, pinned provider), not a resource of the project that is the VPC's boundary (the experimental stackit_vpc_routing_table, pinned provider: project_id, vpc_id, is a VPC-scoped table that the draft does not write and that nothing establishes as the counterpart of an AWS route table), so which networks the table governs is decided by the Network Area the person puts the project in and the routing_table_id each network is given. A table the draft does not make (count or for_each) and one left to the architect are left for review. Several VPCs, a VPC count that is not visible, or a vpc_id (this table's or another unit's) that is a literal, a variable, a data source or an expression the scan cannot reduce to the estate's VPC: which routing domain the table belongs to is the architect's decision. Held critical: it decides which networks, clients or endpoints reach the resource or what it reaches. Closes when: A person decides it as that finding asks, and the finding's closing evidence or decision is recorded. A person decides which STACKIT networks the routing table governs: the draft's custom table is organisation and Network Area scoped, so the Network Area the project joins and the routing table each network is given decide it.

- **Classification:** RECONFIGURE · **Confidence:** MEDIUM · rule `aws.subnet/subnet`

The scan cannot say whether this subnet can start an IPv4 connection to the internet: which route table it follows, or what that table's default route goes to, is not proven; or the route table sends 0.0.0.0/0 to an internet gateway but the scan shows no public IPv4 address for what is launched in the subnet - no server in it that has one (a public address asked for or given by the subnet at launch, or an Elastic IP), a server in it that has none, a server whose address or whose subnet is not visible or not resolved, a server with an address in only some of the instances of a repeated subnet, or a workload the reader does not read (a function, a container service, a cluster): "The instances in the public subnet must have public IP addresses or Elastic IP addresses to enable communication with the internet over the internet gateway" (AWS VPC User Guide, Internet gateways). The stackit_network the draft makes of it (an aws_default_subnet gets none: adopting the account's default subnet is a decision) keeps STACKIT's default gateway, which reaches the internet through source NAT ("Every subnet is routed to the public internet. Technically this is done by source NAT (SNAT) in combination with masquerading.", docs.stackit.cloud Virtual Network concepts), so where the source has no route to the internet the two are not equivalent. The draft sets neither routed nor no_ipv4_gateway (see the subnet's egress finding: what either does to outbound access is not documented). Closing evidence: the route table the subnet follows and its default route - name the association by an exact reference to the table and to the subnet, with a count that is known - and, for a route to an internet gateway, the public IPv4 address of every server in the subnet named by an exact reference (its own switch, an Elastic IP or the subnet's setting at launch) - or an architect's statement of what the source subnet reaches. A subnet that is not proven to reach the internet is never read as one that does. Here: IPv4 egress reading UNKNOWN (table-not-proven (selects-collapsed)); no route table of the configuration is proven for the subnet.
